## Status icons — the canonical definitions

These six are the ENTIRE vocabulary. `scripts/uat-audit.py` fails the build on any
verdict glyph outside this set, because an uncounted glyph is silently dropped by
`uat-backfill.py` and then reads downstream as "never walked" — which turns an
unreadable verdict into a missing pass. Four rows carried a stray `◑` that way, and
`⏳` dropped thirteen more, before the guard existed.

| icon | name | means | what moves it |
|---|---|---|---|
| `✅` | ⏳ | Every clause in the test case was measured on the CURRENT env and held, with a control showing the check could have come out the other way. | Nothing — until the env is replaced or the clause changes, when it returns to ☐. |
| `❌` | FAIL | Measured on the current env and it did NOT hold. A named, reproducible defect. This is the honest home for anything undelivered — a feature that has not shipped is a FAIL, not a block. | A fix, deployed, then re-walked. |
| `⚠️` | PARTIAL | **Working state only — never a terminal verdict.** Some clauses proven, others not. A test case whose clause does not fully hold is a FAILING test case, so a row may not be REPORTED in this state: resolve it to ✅ or ❌ before a convergence cycle is captured. It exists so a walk-in-progress can be saved mid-flight. | Closing the named residual (→ ✅) or accepting it as the failure it is (→ ❌). |
| `⛔` | SUPERSEDED | The test case cannot be answered as written — its subject no longer exists, or it asserts a value the schema forbids. **Not** a parking space for hard work. | Retire-and-REPLACE, one-for-one, recorded in `uat-retirements.csv`. |
| `☐` | NOTRUN | Never walked on this env. Includes a freshly replaced clause, which must NOT inherit the retired row's verdict. | Walking it. |
| `⏳` | PENDING | **Working state only — never a terminal verdict**, same rule as PARTIAL. Written by `scripts/reset-uat.py` when a new env flushes prior evidence, to mark awaiting-re-walk as distinct from never-walked. A row may not be REPORTED in this state: it is outside the PASS/FAIL vocabulary a reader counts, so a row left here is invisible rather than merely undecided — 3 rows were uncounted that way. | Re-walking on the live env, then ✅ or ❌. |

**The denominator is STONE at 286.** Retiring a row always carries a replacement so
the count never moves; `scripts/uat-snapshot.py` refuses any other total. A
floating denominator makes every percentage incomparable across cycles, and it has
drifted before.

**Evidence is per-env.** A new Sovereign flushes ALL prior evidence, so the score
resets to near-zero on every fresh prov (measured: 133 green 2026-07-30 → 25 on
2026-08-03). A drop across an env boundary is the ledger working; a drop WITHIN one
env is a regression. `scripts/uat-drift-guard.py` refuses a ✅ row that cites a
wiped env.

**Env:** live omantel.biz Sovereign, dep `91dc05917e44d1c1`, 2-region converged (region-a 62/62 HRs · region-b 56/56 HRs, me-east-215-a + me-east-215-b). All verdicts below are against THIS env unless a row carries its own labeled prior-env evidence.

**The single decisive gate:** kom4dc EIP quota is **10** (free 3); a fresh post-fix 2-region prov needs **6** EIPs — a HARD WALL with zero orphans to reclaim. Raising the quota **10 → ≥16** would unblock the 7 roll-gated tickets in one fresh prov. The remaining 6 are Anthropic-credential-walled (2), a real remaining build (1), or destructive-walk-gated (2, need a throwaway env [itself EIP-walled] or founder clearance).

The obvious read of 'region-A carries console-https-uatco, region-B does not' is 'the org-controller runs single-region and never writes region-B'. That is WRONG, and this row's own text contains the counter-evidence: region-B's cilium-gateway-console carries 8 listeners INCLUDING per-Org ones for *.r17probe.omani.homes. A controller that could not reach region-B could not have written those either. So region-B IS reachable and HAS been written; what it holds is a per-Org listener set from an EARLIER Org that was never updated for uatco and never cleaned up for r17probe. Anyone fixing this by 'making the controller multi-region' would be fixing a mechanism that is already there. WHAT THE SOURCE SHOWS. tenant_console_tls.go appends the console-https-<slug>/console-http-<slug> pair idempotently to the SHARED kube-system/cilium-gateway-console; teardown IS wired - teardownTenantNetworking (tenant_networking_teardown.go:112) is called from organization_controller.go:455 and is held open by the dedicated TenantNetworkingFinalizer precisely so the cascade cannot be skipped when no other finalizer is present (#4459). So neither the write nor the delete path is missing code. The r17probe listener surviving an Org whose API record reads state='deleted' is therefore NOT a teardown-logic bug - it is #5426 (Organization delete never runs the finalizer cascade: no REST route, no UI action, so the CR is never deleted and the finalizer never fires). The listener outlives the Org because nothing ever asked it to die. CLASSIFICATION: gap (a) belongs to the #5635 family (single-region resource behind a two-region shared EIP, ~50% fresh-TCP failure) - same mechanism, applied to the console listener instead of the app namespace - and the orphan half is #5426. Not filed as a new issue; a third ticket for a mechanism twice-filed would inflate the backlog without adding a fix. NOTE THE MEASUREMENT DISCIPLINE THIS ROW ALREADY GOT RIGHT and that made the adjudication possible: fresh-TCP curl with --no-keepalive. Under HTTP/2 the browser pins one backend and the same host reads 10/10, which is how a 50% split hides. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 PLATFORM REGRESSION, DATED. Same dated cause as row 87 (#5246). RESULT, measured: ES now reads `Ready=True SecretSynced` and the target Secret carries both keys (`anthropicApiKey`, `credentialsJson`). CONTROL: that same init container is what previously logged `no credentials.json key in Secret` — the identical check now passes, so it discriminates. NOT FLIPPED GREEN: the clause still needs its own live walk; what changed is that the reason it could not be walked is gone. Ground: the clause demanded a SERVER redirect; core/marketplace is a STATIC build whose auth is deliberately client-side, so there is no SSR redirect to observe and the assertion contradicts the shipped contract rather than testing it. NOT deleted — the slot is reused so the denominator stays frozen at 286, and the full swap is in docs/ledger/uat-retirements.csv. Reset to ☐ because a NEW clause has never been walked; inheriting the old row's verdict would be the fabrication this ledger exists to stop. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 WALKED ON THE REPLACEMENT CLAUSE - FAILS, and the mechanism is architectural rather than a bug in the page. A live unredeemed voucher was taken from GET /api/v1/org/billing/vouchers/list (26 available, plan m, 50 OMR) and its redeem URL opened in the SAME browser that held the authed owner session. RESULT: the browser stayed on marketplace/redeem/ - redirectCount 0, no bounce to /dashboard - and the STRANGER flow rendered: 1 form, 1 voucher-code input, a 'Sign up to redeem' button, and the voucher-valid copy. The owner identity appears nowhere on the page. SESSION CONTROL, which is what makes this a finding instead of an expired cookie: navigating straight back to the console seconds later returned GET /api/v1/whoami = 200 with email emrah.baysal@openova.io and tier `owner`. The session was live the whole time. MECHANISM, measured not argued: document.cookie is EMPTY on the console's OWN origin, so the catalyst_session cookie is HttpOnly. The marketplace is a separate host running a STATIC build with CLIENT-side auth, and client-side JS cannot read an HttpOnly cookie - so a static marketplace page has no readable signal that the visitor is the owner, on this or any other Sovereign. The owner bounce cannot happen through that path BY CONSTRUCTION, not by oversight. This is the same conclusion the retired clause reached from the opposite direction: the retired version demanded a SERVER redirect, which a static build cannot perform; the replacement demanded a CLIENT bounce, which an HttpOnly session cannot trigger. Both fail for the same underlying reason, so this is a real product gap and not a mis-stated assertion: an owner opening a redeem link is shown a signup form. TO CLOSE: the marketplace needs a readable owner signal - a non-HttpOnly hint cookie scoped to the parent domain, or a whoami call from the marketplace origin with CORS credentials - and the row then asserts the bounce against that. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: NEEDS-CODE — no fix exists yet. This row is engineering work, not a walk; a re-walk cannot change it. ‖ RE-ADJUDICATED 2026-08-10 — NO FURTHER ACTION, LEFT RED. This slot was already retired-and-replaced today AND then walked on the replacement clause, which failed for a MEASURED architectural reason (an HttpOnly session cookie plus a static marketplace origin, with a live whoami=200 as the session control). A second rewrite would be re-wording a clause that has already been re-worded once and then tested; the residual is product work, not ledger work. |

ROOT CAUSE, corrected from the ticket: #5434 diagnosed this as 'the backend never inverts the producer's parameters.databases[].consumer map'. That is not what was wrong. The inverter ALREADY EXISTED and was correct — dependsOnFromHRGraph reads the HelmRelease's Flux dependsOn graph and resolves each target's declared Context entry — but it ran ONLY inside synthesiseAppFromHelmRelease, which is gated on the Application CR being ABSENT. A bootstrap app like spine-harbor HAS a CR (spec {bootstrap:true, helmRelease:{name:bp-harbor, namespace:flux-system}}), so it took HandleApplicationGet, found no spec.dependsOn, and returned empty while the working resolver sat one branch away. The fix is a fallback that reaches the existing resolver, NOT a second derivation: inverting the producer map was declined deliberately because two ways to compute one edge is how the CR path and the HR path drifted apart into this bug in the first place. Guarded by an AST test on the CALL SITE rather than the helper — the defect was never a wrong resolver, it was a resolver nothing called, which is the shape a behaviour test cannot see. Mutation-verified: deleting the call site, removing the delegation, and stripping the rationale comment each turn a test RED. Re-walk this row after the image rolls. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 VERDICT PROMOTED FROM ⚠️ — deploy-gate CONFIRMED by two-way ancestry. An un-title-cased enum is a cosmetic miss; a mangled identifier is wrong data. So the walk's finding - 'the CR and API both say tier=org with the plan carried separately as planSlug=m' - is not a residual at all. It is the model working exactly as #4292 specified, and the walk even confirmed the isolation is BACKED rather than claimed (statefulset.apps/vcluster 1/1 in ns uatco, planSlug=m clearing the M+ tier gate). They are layered in only as STATUS: AppsPage.tsx:130-134 comments it exactly — 'Layer in live status from /api/v1/sovereign/apps so the AppCards reflect ground truth'. THIS NEEDS ADJUDICATION, NOT A UNILATERAL REWRITE: /apps currently answers 'what CAN this Sovereign run', and the row asks it to answer 'what IS running'. Soft-deleted rather than the #5395 orphan class, but the directory and the CR set still do not match 1:1.  ‖ RESIDUAL (a) FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-08-07 (#5819, PR pending; deploy-gated on hw292), AND IT ALSO EXPLAINS RESIDUAL (b). consumptionParentOrg returned the RAW Sovereign FQDN, so showback labelled the parent group 'hw292.omani.works' while the Organization CR, the directory and the treemap all use the slug form 'hw292-omani-works' (spineOrganizationSlug -> orgNamespace). Two spellings of one Org is exactly what this row asserts against. Note this row already recorded it as PERSISTING: an earlier fix removed the literal word 'sovereign' from this path without touching the mismatch underneath, which is why it survived. IT WAS NEVER ONLY COSMETIC. The parent bucket is selected by `org == parentOrg`, where org comes from the openova.io/organization NAMESPACE LABEL, and label values written by the org-controller are CR slugs - DNS-1123, dots impossible. An FQDN-shaped parentOrg could therefore never match anything, so the parent group rendered at 0 cost units on a Sovereign plainly running workloads. That is residual (b): this row's own observation that showback emits the parent at 0 while the treemap Organization layer has no parent node. ONE root cause, TWO symptoms - they were recorded as separate residuals. FIX: route through orgNamespace(), the canonical org->identifier seam (namespace_ensure.go), the same one the self-org CR producer uses. The 'sovereign' fallback for an unresolvable deployment is kept deliberately - it is VISIBLY synthetic, and inventing a plausible-LOOKING slug for an Org we cannot identify would read as a real Organization on the panel. 5 guards, mutation-verified: reverting to the FQDN turns 3 RED; re-deriving inline as strings.ReplaceAll(fqdn,'.','-') turns 2 RED; deleting the reasoning comment turns 1 RED. The AGREEMENT test is the load-bearing one - it compares against spineOrganizationSlug, the producer, NOT a hand-written literal, because a literal lets the two drift the moment either derivation changes. The SEAM test is why the inline mutation exists: ReplaceAll gets hw292.omani.works right and passes a naive behaviour test, then diverges on orgNamespace's dash-run collapse (it fails on a--b..c.example), which is precisely how the two derivations drifted apart to begin with. Residual NEW (ii) - GET /api/v1/organizations returning 3 records against 2 Organization CRs - is untouched and still open. ‖ RESIDUAL (ii) FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-08-07 (#5833, PR pending; deploy-gated on hw292). '3 records where only 2 Organization CRs exist' is mergeOrgResponses keying on Subdomain ALONE. Two holes, both producing a DUPLICATE rather than a miss - which is why the symptom was an inflated count and not a disappearing Org: (1) the CR side ALWAYS sets Subdomain to the slug (orgCRToResponse) while the store side sets whatever the provision record carries, and a custom-domain Org (DomainMode != free-subdomain) legitimately has NO pool subdomain, so one Organization gets two different keys; (2) an EMPTY key skipped dedupe ENTIRELY - both loops appended without registering anything, so a store row with no subdomain and its own CR row both landed. FIX: two rows are the same Org when they match on EITHER axis (OrganizationID or Subdomain), both registered on insert, axes namespaced id:/sub: so a subdomain equal to another Org's tenant id cannot merge two DIFFERENT Organizations. A row identifiable on neither axis is KEPT but explicitly excluded from dedupe rather than silently treated as deduped. THE CORRECTION WORTH CARRYING: my first cut keyed on OrganizationID with Subdomain as fallback. ALL SIX TARGETED TESTS PASSED. The FULL suite then went red on a pre-existing case - a store record carrying uuid-acme and the CR for the same slug acme have DIFFERENT ids, so id-first split one Organization into two, mirroring the very defect being fixed. Six green targeted tests are not evidence that an invariant holds, only evidence about the six things they check; the 254s full suite is what caught it. That invariant is now pinned by TestMergeOrgResponses_SameSlugDifferentIDsIsOneOrg. 7 guards, mutation-verified 4 ways, plus controls that a too-AGGRESSIVE key would drop the funnel-only rows #4479 added this merge to surface. Gates: go vet clean, full handler suite 254s green. This is a determinate state, not a partial: no source work remains and it closes on the next prov. hw292 cannot receive it — the cutover chart wants 0.1.174, has 0.1.159 applied, and cannot upgrade because the upgrade needs the registry path that is itself broken. Clauses (a),(b),(ii) are deploy-gated; clause (i) inherits row 15's producer wall below and will NOT close on a roll. ‖ NO-PARTIALS DISPOSITION 2026-08-10 — RECLASSIFIED ⚠️ -> ❌. CORRECTS AN EARLIER STAMP OF MINE FROM THE SAME DAY. I first recorded that the console does NOT silently re-authenticate from a live keycloak realm session. THAT IS WRONG - the mechanism works, and my test never invoked it. RE-RUN, identical except one step: signed in via PIN, traversed grafana to prime the realm session, dropped ONLY catalyst_session (keeping all four realm cookies AUTH_SESSION_ID, KC_AUTH_SESSION_HASH, KEYCLOAK_IDENTITY, KEYCLOAK_SESSION), CLEARED sessionStorage, reloaded the bare console URL -> lands on /dashboard with ZERO email inputs, signed in as the owner, with freshly minted oidc access/id/refresh tokens. WHAT THE FIRST RUN MEASURED: I had not cleared sessionStorage, so catalyst:authed=1 survived from the initial login while the cookie was gone. router.tsx calls this the 'stale-marker fast path' and notes the layout 'discovers the 401 when the stale-marker fast path has bypassed this gate' - so the app took the fast path, hit a 401 and dropped to the PIN wall WITHOUT attempting silent SSO. I saw the PIN wall and blamed the mechanism; the mechanism never ran. THE ROW STILL FAILS, for a narrower and better reason: a genuine TTL expiry produces exactly the state my first run created by accident - the cookie expires on the server clock while catalyst:authed=1 persists for the tab's lifetime - so a real re-entry hits the fast path, 401s and PIN-walls, while the silent path that demonstrably works never fires. The defect is 'when catalyst:authed is stale relative to the session cookie, the fast path bypasses silent SSO instead of falling back to it', tracked on #5887. Evidence docs/sessions/2026-08-08/hw292-ttl/hw292-silent-reauth-works.png. ‖ hw292-2026-08-08 — FIX WRITTEN AND MERGED THIS SESSION (#5909), delivery blocked by the same source-level freeze as the rest. Root cause established earlier today: router.tsx:287 'if (hasCatalystSession()) return' short-circuits BEFORE line 290's probeWhoamiAndCacheMarker, so #5460's 401-revocation was unreachable from the one gate that needed it — the stale marker was self-perpetuating at the only site that could clear it, and the #3374 Layer-B silent-SSO leg was skipped with it. #5909 gives the marker a written-at stamp that ages out after 5 min; missing, non-numeric and future stamps all read stale, and all four production write sites now go through markAuthed() so no call site can mint an un-ageing marker. Gates at merge: 62 auth-gate tests, 229 files / 2121 tests full UI suite, tsc clean. WHY IT IS STILL ❌: the console is delivered by bp-catalyst-platform, which reads Ready=False DependencyNotReady — inside the 51-HelmRelease cascade rooted at bp-cilium SourceNotReady, 'no auth config for ghcr.io in the docker-registry Secret ghcr-pull' (#5759). Source-correct and unwalked; resolves on the fresh prov, not on more code. This completes the ❌ sweep: all 14 failing rows now carry a 2026-08-08 walk. ‖ hw292-2026-08-09 BROWSER WALK, HALF PROVEN — STAYS ❌. Playwright, fresh context, no session: GET https://console.hw292.omani.works/ redirects to /login and PAINTS a real PIN wall — h1 'Sign in', 'Enter your email to receive a 6-digit PIN', the origin echoed back, an email textbox, and a Send code button that is DISABLED until an address is entered. No dashboard shell, no stale signed-in state. Screenshot row29-console-unauth-login.png. WHY THIS IS NOT GREEN: the defect #5909 fixed is hasCatalystSession() returning true off a marker with NO catalyst:authed-at stamp, short-circuiting the whoami probe at router.tsx:287. A FRESH browser never had a marker, so this walk cannot separate 'the staleness check works' from 'the staleness check never had to run' — it is vacuous against the actual clause. Closing it needs a stale marker INJECTED into sessionStorage (key catalyst:authed='1' with catalyst:authed-at absent) followed by a reload, asserting the PIN wall still wins. Refusing to stamp green on a control that cannot fail. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: ENV-STATE — needs an environment SHAPE this Sovereign does not have (pre-cutover window, single region, or a destructive prov->wipe cycle). Not a code gap and not re-walkable here.|

The Continuum CR backing it also exists and is healthy: `shared-data/dr-shared-pg` phase=Healthy (8 Continuum CRs total on the Sovereign, all Healthy). So the DR substrate this row requires is genuinely there. **What is missing is the single status key the control gates on:** every one of the 8 Continuum CRs reports `status.replicaPromotable` = UNSET. That is #5601 — the reader at `continuum_dr_extras.go` consumes `status.replicaPromotable`, a key the Continuum controller never writes, so the field keeps its Go zero value and the Switchover control reads not-armed while the standby is demonstrably caught up. A regression test already encodes the contradiction (`continuum_dr_extras_5601_test.go:83` — 'the CR says standbyAvailable=true/phase=Healthy/lag=0 and the live replica is Ready'). METHOD NOTE, because it nearly produced a false finding: my first probe used group `continuums.continuum.openova.io` and returned nothing, which reads as 'no Continuum CRs' — the real group is `continuums.dr.openova.io`. A zero from a wrong-group query is indistinguishable from a real absence, so the count above is quoted only after listing the CRD. Row closes when #5601 lands and the control arms against this same live pair — no new DR infrastructure is required.  FIX MERGED 2026-08-06 (#5601, PR #5780): the promotability derivation now consumes status.standbyAvailable + status.phase (the keys the Continuum controller actually writes) instead of status.replicationHealthy, which it never writes — that probe left the zero-value false and disarmed the button over a healthy pair. Mutation-proven both directions. A SECOND unguarded half was found and closed in the same PR: phaseReady accepts Healthy OR FailedOver, and deleting the FailedOver clause left all 8 tests green — so after a switchover the failback control could regress to this row's exact symptom in the one state where an operator cannot recover without it. Disambiguated the same way G3 was — an empty listing alone cannot tell 'no instances' from 'unknown kind', so the CRD presence was checked explicitly. The Postgres underneath is entirely healthy: shared-pg, shared-pg-b, shared-pg-c all instances=3/readyInstances=3 'Cluster in healthy state', plus cnpg/cnpg-pair-bp-cnpg-pair-primary 3/3 — but that last one is a CNPG *Cluster* NAMED cnpg-pair-*, NOT a CnpgPair CR. The naming makes them easy to conflate; they are different kinds. UNIFIED FINDING with G3: BOTH dr.openova.io CRDs (continuums + cnpgpairs) are installed with zero instances while the underlying Postgres pairs are live. That is exactly the #4986/#4987 shape — a live DR pair with no DR CR above it — and it explains row 57 and G3 with ONE cause rather than two. So the CRD is shipped, RBAC granted, cache wired, reader implemented and continuum-controller deployed 1/1 (5d14h) — with no write path. The consumers were built against CRs nothing emits. NAMING TRAP worth recording: cnpg/cnpg-pair-bp-cnpg-pair-primary is 3/3 healthy, but it is a CNPG *Cluster* NAMED cnpg-pair-*, NOT a CnpgPair CR — different kinds, near-identical names, and reading it as the pairing object makes this row look satisfied when its dependency does not exist. HEALTH HALF PASSES and is sampled per region, not once: grafana pods are 3/3 Running in region-a AND region-b, and a grep over the last 300 lines of all containers returns 'cannot resolve write host' count 0 in region-a and 0 in region-b - no crashloop. CONSOLE HALF IMPROVED: /app/bp-grafana now resolves to the platform component (data-testid app-detail-namespace=flux-system, app-detail-phase=Ready), header and Overview both read STATUS Ready, and the Topology tab enumerates BOTH regions explicitly as live cards - 'hw-me-east-215-a-rtz-prod . 1c56518035a83e03 . mgmt / PRIMARY / serves writes' and 'hw-me-east-215-b-rtz-prod . 1c56518035a83e03-me-east-215-b-1 . mgmt / PRIMARY / serves writes', Pattern: active-active, both cluster ids populated. HELD AT PARTIAL, not upgraded: the row asks for a per-region Healthy/Running verdict and what renders is a single aggregate Ready plus a per-region PLACEMENT ROLE; the dedicated Status panel still reads verbatim 'n/a - bootstrap component (HelmRelease, no Application CR). Live rollout status is tracked via Flux.' - which is exactly the hw291 residual, unchanged. Cause is structural and unfixed on main: bootstrap-kit HRs have no Application CR, so GET .../applications/bp-grafana/status returns 404 application-not-found and there is no per-region health to render. Category (a): no merged-on-main fix found for this leg.  ROOT CAUSE UNIFIED 2026-08-07 (source read, no env needed) — this row's residual is NOT specific to its app, and it is the SAME cause as rows 19/188. The Status panel string the walk quotes verbatim is emitted at products/catalyst/bootstrap/ui/src/pages/sovereign/AppDetail/TopologyTab.tsx:1045-1047, gated on `isBootstrap`: when the component is a bootstrap-kit HelmRelease with no Application CR, the panel renders 'n/a — bootstrap component (HelmRelease, no Application CR). Live rollout status is tracked via Flux.' unconditionally. So the per-region HEALTH verdict this row asks for is not missing because of anything wrong with grafana or keycloak — the data plane measured GREEN in both regions (pods Running per region, zero matching error lines per region). It is missing because the Application-keyed status surface deliberately declines to speak for bootstrap components, which have no Application CR to speak about. ONE CAUSE, FOUR ROWS: 19 and 188 (the apps grid is keyed on the blueprint CATALOG, so no Application CR renders as its own card) and 67 and 69 (the app-detail Status panel abstains for bootstrap components). Both are the same structural fact — bootstrap-kit components are HelmReleases WITHOUT Application CRs, so every Application-keyed surface degrades for exactly that set. Fixing one without the other would move a symptom. NOT PATCHING THIS UNILATERALLY: the honest fix is either (a) synthesise Application CRs for bootstrap components so the whole Application-keyed surface works uniformly, or (b) give the Status panel a HelmRelease-backed per-region health path so it stops abstaining. (a) changes the object model; (b) changes what 'Status' means. Both are founder-level design calls, and the row cannot go green until one is chosen. ‖ THE SINGLE REMAINING RESIDUAL IS FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-08-07 (#5835, PR pending; deploy-gated on hw292), and it was the SAME defect in rows 67 and 69. The Status panel reading 'n/a - bootstrap component (HelmRelease, no Application CR)' is not a UI copy choice: HandleApplicationStatus read the Application CR AND NOTHING ELSE, so a bootstrap-kit component (an HR with no companion CR) got a hard 404, while its SIBLING GET /applications/{name} returned a complete synthesised answer (synthesiseAppFromHelmRelease, and since #5827 synthesiseAppFromRuntime). The console reacted the only way it could - TopologyTab.tsx:144 DISABLES the poll entirely for isBootstrap - and printed the n/a string. So one page contradicted itself: this row's own walk measured the header and Overview reading STATUS Ready, from the synthesised detail response, while the Status panel three inches below said there was no status to report. The data existed the whole time; only that endpoint refused to project it. FIX: /status gets the same fallback chain as the detail endpoint, HR first then runtime. It DELEGATES rather than re-deriving - computing a phase from the HelmRelease here would be a THIRD derivation of one component's status, and two was already enough for the detail and status paths to disagree in front of an operator. ORDERING IS GUARDED: runtime-first would reduce a bootstrap component's declared identity to observed Pods on this endpoint while the detail endpoint still reports it - the same disagreement in the other direction. No spec is invented (no CR means no spec.placement; the Topology tab derives real placement from live Pods via .../placement, #3982). 4 guards, mutation-verified: delete the call site; drop the runtime fall-through; flip the ordering. A CLAIM I RETRACTED BEFORE SHIPPING: my first conditions test asserted the key is always PRESENT on the wire. The struct tag says otherwise - conditions carries omitempty, so an empty slice is omitted entirely, on the CR-backed path as much as this one. Callers tolerate an absent key; what they cannot tolerate is null where a list is expected. The stronger assertion would have pinned a contract the existing code does not honour and broken the CR path this change does not touch. NOT CLAIMED: the FE isBootstrap gate is NOT removed - that is a separate change and should follow a live re-walk confirming the endpoint now answers. This row stays partial until then. Gates: go vet clean, full handler suite rc=0 254s; noted honestly, an immediately prior identical run reported FAIL at 253s with no capturable failure lines, so there is an unidentified flake in the suite and this is ONE clean run, not a verified-stable claim. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 PROMOTED ⚠️ → ❌ — deploy-gated on the API half, but a REAL FRONT-END WALL remains that a roll cannot clear. #5836 makes `/status` answer, yet `TopologyTab.tsx:144` still disables the poll when `isBootstrap`, and `:1045-1047` HARD-RENDERS the literal `n/a — bootstrap component` string regardless of what the API returns. Both lines are still on main today. So the row needs the front-end un-gated, not merely the image rolled. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: DEPLOY-GATED — the fix is MERGED and simply not running on this env. Re-walking here reproduces this same FAIL; it closes on a roll or a fresh prov, not on more measurement.|

CONTROL PROVING IT IS THE CHECKOUT LEG, NOT MAIL: the CONSOLE PIN path delivered to this same mailbox 90 min earlier in this session — 3 independent codes, same IMAP reader, each verify 200 + whoami 200 (row 235, green today). Mail works, mailbox works, console issuance works; marketplace checkout issuance does not. The marketplace pin/issue API returns 200 and the UI reacts to that success, so the failure is AFTER the API answers and is silent. DELIBERATELY DID NOT type the 96-min-old code — it is past the stated 10-min expiry and would have thrown 'code invalid', mis-attributing the defect to verification when it is issuance/delivery one step earlier. Render half (no password field, correct copy) still holds. Rows 20 and 23 are downstream — they need checkout driven through to create a 2nd Organization. ‖ hw292-2026-08-09 REFINED — endpoint identified and REGRESSION WINDOW BOUNDED. Checkout sign-in does NOT use /api/v1/auth/pin/issue (the console path) — it uses POST /auth/magic-link. Traced my own click in the gateway access log: '10:05:39 POST /auth/magic-link status=200 duration=3.794s'. That ~3.8s is SMTP-shaped latency, so the handler attempts a send, takes ~4s, and reports success regardless. NOT a never-worked: the same log holds a COMPLETE successful chain two days earlier — 2026-08-07 22:58:40 magic-link 200 (3.76s) -> 22:58:48 /auth/verify 200 -> /auth/me 200. A code was issued, delivered, read and accepted on this same Sovereign. WORKING 2026-08-07 22:58, BROKEN 2026-08-09 10:05. Next look: catalyst-system/marketplace-api holds SMTP_HOST/PORT/USER/PASS + FROM_EMAIL, all via valueFrom not literals, and its logs contain ZERO mail/smtp lines in 300 entries despite a 3.8s send — so either the secret those resolve from is empty/stale, or the send error is swallowed. The swallowed-error shape is worse: an issuance path returning 200 on a failed send looks healthy to every monitor while no customer can ever buy. ‖ hw292-2026-08-09 ROOT CAUSE IS A SOVEREIGNTY TETHER (#5921). marketplace-api sends the customer sign-in code via smtp-host=mail.openova.io:587, resolved from Secret/sovereign-smtp-credentials with all five keys present and non-empty — so it is wired deliberately, not config drift. hw292 is POST-CUTOVER with cutoverComplete=true, meaning a Sovereign asserted independent of the OpenOva mothership still needs OUR mail server for every customer sign-in. ADR-0002 / DOD Pillar 5 enumerate EIGHT tethers that cutover pivots; outbound customer-auth SMTP is not among them, so no step pivots it and no check looks for it. Step-08's deny-egress proof holds github.com / ghcr.io / harbor.openova.io for 600s — mail.openova.io is not in that set and no reconcile needs SMTP, so the hold passes with this tether fully live. Same shape as the 62 ghcr.io HelmRepositories that survived the same proof (#5919). NOT ISOLATED: a CiliumNetworkPolicy/baseline-default-deny is in force and egress denial to :587 is consistent with everything measured, but the pod is distroless so I could not dial from inside it; the alternative is a swallowed send error. Either way the tether is the thing that should not exist, and it materially qualifies the Pillar-5 green. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: NEEDS-CODE — no fix exists yet. This row is engineering work, not a walk; a re-walk cannot change it.|

This row asserted the limiter is 'known-good from source'; that assertion is now confirmed line-by-line rather than repeated: (a) core/services/gateway/ratelimit.go:90 path-scopes the burst to strings.HasPrefix(r.URL.Path, "/api/billing/vouchers/redeem") — a dedicated short-window key, separate from the global rpm; (b) THE THRESHOLD MATCHES THE ROW'S OWN WORDING: main.go:29-30 default REDEEM_RATE_LIMIT=5 per REDEEM_RATE_WINDOW_SEC=10, per IP, so 'after ~5 attempts' is the configured behaviour and not an approximation the walker has to interpret. The env vars are set NOWHERE in the chart, which is fine and worth stating explicitly so nobody reads the absence as 'unconfigured': the CODE defaults are the active values, and redeemLimit<=0 (which would disable the burst limit entirely) is not reachable from that default; (c) the UI half is landed WITH tests — redeem.astro:89,180,185 read the body and headers up front so the 429 notice carries the server's retry_after, guarded by core/marketplace/src/lib/rateLimitNotice.test.ts and redeemMarkupContract.test.ts. ⚠ WALK HAZARD, record before walking: ratelimit.go:40 states the limiter FAILS OPEN — 'If Valkey is unavailable, requests are allowed through'. So a burst that produces no 429 is ambiguous between 'limiter broken' and 'Valkey down', exactly the same shape as the 401-before-limiter trap this row already avoided. The authenticated walk MUST carry a Valkey-reachable control (e.g. confirm the global rpm still trips, or read the gateway's Valkey connection state) before recording any verdict from an absent 429. This is the only row examined today whose stated reason survived checking intact — 212/213 cited the wrong issue, 5/6/10/11 rested on a reading taken 90 minutes too early, R17 named an RBAC gap that was already granted, and row 5 demanded a CRD-forbidden value. ‖ hw292-2026-08-08 RE-PROBE — still inconclusive, and now we know WHY, which the earlier walk did not establish. 8 rapid POSTs to /api/v1/auth/pin/issue with the same address returned HTTP 422 on every attempt. CONTROL: the same POST with a DIFFERENT address ALSO returned 422. That control is what makes the run readable — the limiter is per-email (auth.go:538, the rate-limit check runs BEFORE EnsureUser), so a distinct address would have been unaffected if the 422s were rate-limiting. They were not: every request was rejected UPSTREAM of the limiter, so the probe never reached the surface this row tests. Without the control this reads as '8 attempts, no rate-limit notice, row confirmed failing' — confidently wrong. NOT ESTABLISHED: what the 422 signifies (probably an unprovisioned principal; the response body was not captured and I am not asserting it). TO ACTUALLY WALK IT: a VALID principal is required, and hammering the owner address 8x would burn the session-minting path the rest of the walk depends on — so it needs a throwaway provisioned Org user, or better the checkout/redeem surface this row's wording actually names rather than the PIN endpoint I substituted for it. ‖ hw292-2026-08-08 SOURCE RESOLVED — the fix for this row EXISTS, is purpose-built for it, and is ENABLED BY DEFAULT. core/services/gateway/ratelimit.go:21 is commented '#3376 row-92' and adds a redeem-specific burst limit precisely because the global rpm is too lenient: '120/min = 2/s lets a 5-in-3s burst sail through'. Wiring confirmed end to end: main.go:29-30 redeemRateLimit := getEnvInt('REDEEM_RATE_LIMIT', 5) and redeemRateWindow := getEnvInt('REDEEM_RATE_WINDOW_SEC', 10); main.go:120 passes both into NewRateLimiter. Default is therefore 5 requests per 10s per IP on a dedicated short-window path-scoped key — which matches this row's clause ('after ~5 attempts a rate-limit notice appears; a single legitimate redeem is unaffected') almost exactly, including the path-scoping that keeps one honest redeem clean. redeemLimit <= 0 would disable it; the default is 5, so it is ON. CORRECTS MY OWN EARLIER WALKS: both prior attempts probed /api/v1/auth/pin/issue, which is the WRONG SURFACE — this limiter lives in the marketplace GATEWAY on the redeem/checkout path, not on the PIN endpoint, and the customer funnel lives in core/marketplace per the four-front-end-trees rule. My 8x422 result was a measurement of the wrong door. Row is DEPLOY-GATED, not missing code: exercising it needs a gateway reachable with the redeem path live, which the 51-HR ghcr-pull cascade (#5759) currently prevents. ‖ hw292-2026-08-09 WALKED THE RIGHT SURFACE AT LAST — CONFIRMED FAIL. Route taken from source, not guessed: ratelimit.go:90 gates on prefix /api/billing/vouchers/redeem, and main.go:96 exposes /api/billing/vouchers/redeem-preview as Public. Fired 8 rapid POSTs from one IP: ALL EIGHT returned HTTP 404 'voucher not valid' — each reached the billing service; NOT ONE 429. Expected 5 per 10s per IP (REDEEM_RATE_LIMIT default 5, REDEEM_RATE_WINDOW_SEC 10). CONTROL: the storefront stayed HTTP 200 across the same window, so this is not a blanket outage and the burst really did land. THIS SUPERSEDES MY OWN EARLIER STAMP TODAY: I read the source, saw the #3376-row-92 limiter wired in main.go with a non-zero default, and recorded that it 'exists and is on by default'. Source-present is NOT runtime-armed — the running gateway does not enforce it. Candidate causes NOT yet distinguished: (a) the deployed gateway image predates the limiter, (b) REDEEM_RATE_LIMIT is overridden to <=0 in the chart, (c) the Valkey backing store the limiter counts in is unreachable so it fails OPEN. I fired 8 SEQUENTIAL POSTs at /api/billing/vouchers/redeem-preview, got 8x404 and no 429, and concluded the limiter was 'in source but not armed at runtime'. That was a measurement error: sequential curl invocations are spaced by their own round-trip, so 8 of them never exceed 5-per-10s. Re-run with 20 CONCURRENT requests: 5x HTTP 429 + rest 404. The limiter IS armed and firing, with the verbatim ratelimit.go body 'too many redeem attempts — please wait a few seconds and try again', retry_after 10. CONTROL: 10 rapid GET /api/catalog/plans in the same window returned 200x10, so the 429 is path-scoped, not a global throttle. FAIL-OPEN HAZARD ALSO RULED OUT: I had reported VALKEY_ADDR as empty — it is a configMapKeyRef, so .value is empty BY CONSTRUCTION; it resolves to valkey-primary.valkey.svc.cluster.local:6379 via cm/org-services-config and valkey-primary-0 is 1/1. The 400 is the control that makes it conclusive. METHOD NOTE: a single probe returned 000 for uatco and 200 for walk-stranger-two, which invited 'uatco is broken, the other is fine' — ten probes each show BOTH at exactly 50%, so one probe would have produced a confidently wrong per-host verdict. Fixed here: the claim set is extracted into buildHandoverSessionClaims() and stamps pinIssuer(). THE CONSOLE API IS ALIVE — stated as a correction to my own earlier reading in this same pass, which is the part worth carrying forward. Single-shot probes made it look dead: /healthz, /sovereign/ and /api/v1/whoami each returned 503 `no healthy upstream`, and I wrote that a handover redirect would be judged by a dead API. Re-sampled 20x per path over FRESH TCP (curl --no-keepalive --http1.1), the real shape is a HALF-UNHEALTHY VIP with a per-connection split on EVERY path — / 11/20 served, /sovereign/jobs 10/20, /healthz 9/20, /sovereign/ 10/20 — and on a served connection /api/v1/whoami answers 401 unauthenticated, i.e. alive and discriminating. One sample per path against a ~50 percent split is a coin flip, and it produced a confident per-PATH story that re-sampling refutes. One does now: Job `syft-grype-…-29763030` is `Failed` with `BackoffLimitExceeded` at startTime 18:34:03Z. The Jobs feed's leaf `task-syft-sbom` execution carries the IDENTICAL startedAt and reads `status: running, finishedAt: null`, and its parent group `reconcilers` therefore reads `running` — with **0 failed leaves out of 183**. THE DEFECT: the Day-2 scanner collapse never TERMINATES a failed execution, so a failed Job reads running forever and the group inherits the optimistic state. CONTROL, which locates the defect precisely: the rollup CAN emit non-succeeded states — `cutover` reads succeeded/14 and `reconcilers` reads running — so this is scanner-collapse ingestion (helmwatch/reconcilers.go:64-90), not the rollup at store.go:580. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: NEEDS-CODE — no fix exists yet. This row is engineering work, not a walk; a re-walk cannot change it. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 STORE HALF FIXED - the 08-10 re-walk above named the ingestion layer, and it was only half the chain. VERIFIED FIRST, not assumed: the ingestion fix HAS landed (a63337dd3 / #5931 is an ancestor of origin/main; adoptRunHeadline at helmwatch/reconcilers.go:175 now resolves all three collapse sites, and its suite is green), so the recency defect this row previously described is no longer true on main and was not re-fixed. What still failed is one layer down, in the STORE bridge, and it reproduces the live symptom exactly. (A) jobs/reconciler_bridge.go seeded a cron leaf runs in LIST order and StartExecution stamps Job.Status=running on every allocation, so whichever run was processed LAST left the headline - an in-flight run listed after a failed one turned the leaf, and therefore the group, `running`. The ObsKindTask branch already re-asserted a resolved status with a comment naming this exact hazard; the ObsKindCron branch simply had no such line. (B) seedCronExecutionsLocked deduped by run name and left an already-recorded run UNTOUCHED, so a run first observed in flight was written running/FinishedAt=nil and never terminated when it later failed - verbatim the hw292 reading of `task-syft-sbom` carrying the failed Job startedAt while reporting `status: running, finishedAt: null`. Both are fixed with failing-first tests in reconciler_bridge_stale_run_164_test.go, each with a control that answers the other way (the task leaf passes unfixed, locating A in the cron branch; an in-flight run re-observed twice must STAY running, so the terminate path cannot fire on everything it re-sees). A first attempt at (A) re-asserted the producer status unconditionally and was caught by the pre-existing TestReconcilerBridge_FailingCron_RedWhileInstallGreen, because a cron leaf status is its latest RUN and not the producer - the shipped fix folds the runs through resolveRunHeadline instead. NOT FLIPPED GREEN: this is source work delivered against a named live symptom, and the row owes a re-walk on an env carrying it. ‖ hw293-2026-08-10 NOT MEASURABLE ON THE CURRENT ENV — verdict and walk stamp deliberately UNCHANGED; nothing was re-measured and no new claim is made. Recorded only so the next walker does not mistake an absence for the defect this row names. On hw293 there are ZERO cutover Job objects to form a group at all: bp-self-sovereign-cutover@0.1.177 never installed, `create: failed to create: Secret "sh.helm.release.v1.self-sovereign-cutover.v1" is invalid: data: Too long: must have at most 1048576 bytes`, first 13:04:38Z and last 13:17:56Z, #6004. THE DISTINCTION THAT MATTERS AND THAT THIS NOTE EXISTS TO PRESERVE: "no cutover group exists" is a DIFFERENT observation from "a group with a failed child reads a fake Succeeded", and collapsing the first into the second would charge a chart-size fault to the rollup code this row is actually about — a failure attributed to the wrong cause is worse than an unwalked row. Neither half was sampled in any case, since a group needs children to roll up. NOTE ON THE EDGE, corrected inside this pass: the console VIP is half-unhealthy — about half of all FRESH-TCP connections get envoy 503 `no healthy upstream` on EVERY path (20x each with curl --no-keepalive: / 11/20, /sovereign/jobs 10/20, /healthz 9/20, /sovereign/ 10/20), and a served connection answers /api/v1/whoami with 401, so the API is alive. An earlier single-shot reading in this same pass mistook that per-connection split for a per-path outage; one sample against a 50 percent split is a coin flip. It does not bear on this row either way. The store-side work recorded above still owes its re-walk on an env that carries a genuinely FAILED child, and hw293 is not that env.|

SCREENSHOT: [📷 row183-openbao-bare-url-notauthorized.png](../../docs/sessions/2026-08-06/hw292-screenshots/row183-openbao-bare-url-notauthorized.png) (hw292-2026-08-06)  ‖ CANDIDATE ROOT CAUSE FOUND + FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-08-07 (#5829, PR pending; deploy-gated on hw292), and the 08-04-PASS / 08-06-FAIL split on the SAME env is what pointed at it. In the sso-configure reconcile loop the two sys/policies/acl writes were the ONLY writes whose failure was swallowed - `if bao_write ...; then log ...; fi` with NO else - while the auth/oidc/role/operator write SIX LINES BELOW already did `else log WARN; continue`. So a failed policy write did not stop the tick: the role was then written with token_policies=[sso-operator-read], naming a policy that does not exist. OpenBao accepts that role; its tokens carry a dangling policy name and therefore NO capabilities, and every request 403s - including sys/internal/ui/mounts, which is exactly what the UI calls to fill the Secrets Engines panel. That is this row's symptom verbatim: session genuine (final URL /ui/vault/secrets, hasTokenInput=false, identity badge present) while the panel reads 'Not authorized', 3/3 loads, with NO error anywhere because the LOGIN SUCCEEDS. It also explains the date split: the 08-04 walk hit the bare host with no prior session, the 08-06 walk was explicitly POST-SESSION-ESTABLISH, so only the second exercised the OIDC-minted token rather than the bootstrap one. FIX: both policy writes now gate the tick. `continue`, NOT a louder log - the failure mode is ORDERING, a policy must EXIST before a role names it, and writing the role first with the policy 'next tick' leaves a window where the operator is authenticated and powerless. Guard tests/sso-policy-write-gates-tick-5829.sh, mutation-verified (reverting EITHER gate turns it red); it asserts on `continue` INSIDE the extracted write block rather than on the file containing `continue` anywhere (the render has several - a whole-file grep could never go red), opens with a vacuity control proving the script rendered at all, and pins the policy-before-role ORDERING separately because if the role write moves above the policy writes every `continue` becomes decorative. Lockstep 5 sites, bp-openbao 1.2.65->1.2.66 + bp-catalyst-platform 1.4.1329->1.4.1330; check-bootstrap-kit-pin-sync 67 pairs PASS, catalog-seed lockstep 68 checked 0 fail, g117-5-sso-tier1 still PASS. HONEST SCOPE: I could NOT reach hw292's OpenBao to confirm the live token's policy set. The walk's own evidence is consistent with this cause and I found no other path that produces a 403 on that endpoint from a cleanly authenticated session, but this is a SOURCE-DERIVED diagnosis, not a live one, and the row stays partial until re-walked. The fix is unconditionally correct either way: a swallowed write that leaves a role referencing a missing policy is a defect on its own terms. ‖ NO-PARTIALS DISPOSITION 2026-08-10 — RECLASSIFIED ⚠️ -> ❌. Every clause in a test case must hold for the row to pass; this row's evidence NAMES a clause that does not. Only the class changed, from one that reads as 'nearly there' to one that reads as 'does not pass'. Consequence worth knowing, because it is why the drift was invisible: uat-backfill.py computes each row's identity window against the CANON text, so a drifted row's window collapses to zero and it writes NO observations at all -- the row does not appear wrong in uat-raw.csv, it disappears from it, and the retest policy then reads that absence as never-passed. |

The apps grid does not render the Application ESTATE at all; it renders the blueprint CATALOG. AppsPage.tsx:116 builds its card list from `resolveApplications(store.selectedComponents)`, and applicationCatalog.ts:72 iterates BOOTSTRAP_KIT plus the selected component set — i.e. the wizard store's catalog. Application CRs never contribute card IDENTITY. They are layered in only as STATUS: AppsPage.tsx:130-134 comments it exactly — 'Layer in live status from /api/v1/sovereign/apps so the AppCards reflect ground truth'. So the walk's observation (46 bp-* cards, 14 Application CRs, none of them rendering as their own card) is not a discrepancy between two counts; it is one collection being displayed and a different collection being consulted for badges. CONSEQUENCE: this row's assertion ('one card per Application') and the implementation disagree at the design level, so no small patch satisfies it. Making it true means re-keying the grid on the Application list and treating catalog entries as the not-yet-installed remainder — a deliberate UX change, not a bugfix. THIS NEEDS ADJUDICATION, NOT A UNILATERAL REWRITE: /apps currently answers 'what CAN this Sovereign run', and the row asks it to answer 'what IS running'. Both are legitimate products; only the founder should choose. I am deliberately not 'fixing' this by rewriting the grid, because that would silently change an operator surface to satisfy a ledger row. Sibling: row 188 (catalyst-api likewise has no card) is the same cause. ‖ THE UI LEG IS FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-08-07 (#5827, PR pending; deploy-gated on hw292). This row's own evidence contained the contradiction and it took a source read to see it: GET .../applications/catalyst-api/placement answers 200 with a correct single Primary target (this row measured that), while GET .../applications/catalyst-api answers 404 - two endpoints on the SAME path prefix disagreeing about whether the component exists. Cause: catalyst-api is a Deployment rendered BY the bp-catalyst-platform HR, so it owns neither an Application CR nor a same-named HelmRelease and HandleApplicationGet's two lookups both miss; the placement endpoint does not care because it derives from LIVE PODS. So the API simultaneously knew the component and denied it existed depending on the suffix, and the console rendered 'App not found - the component catalyst-api is not part of this deployment' for something plainly running in front of the caller. That is exactly why this row's own subject could not be walked in a browser while its backend clause passed. FIX: a THIRD and last fallback, synthesiseAppFromRuntime, after the CR and the HR both miss. ONE IDENTITY PREDICATE, NOT TWO - existence is decided by podBelongsToComponent, the exact function the placement path uses; re-answering the same question a second way IS the defect, not an implementation detail of it. Readiness likewise reuses the existing podIsReady (I wrote one, found the identical function in dashboard.go, deleted mine). FABRICATES NOTHING: no CR means no uid/blueprint/version/parameters/environmentRef, all left empty rather than guessed; runtimeDerived:true marks the whole answer as an OBSERVATION so a consumer can tell 'declares nothing' from 'declaration not fetched' without inferring it from which fields are blank. Phase is strict - Ready requires EVERY matched Pod ready, because rounding one degraded replica of three up to Ready is how a status panel starts lying. ORDERING IS LOAD-BEARING and guarded: the runtime path is LAST, because running it before the HR lookup would strip every bootstrap component's declared blueprint down to the bare observed shape. 4 guards, mutation-verified. ONE CAUGHT ITSELF and is worth recording: the first omitempty mutation SILENTLY DID NOT APPLY - gofmt had realigned the struct field so the replace matched nothing - and the test 'passed'. A mutation that does not apply is not a passing test, it is an unexecuted mutation. Redone against the real text; it then went red as intended. Gates: go vet clean, full handler suite 254s green. NOT FIXED HERE: this does not give bootstrap components Application CRs. THE ENTRY SURFACE IS HALF-DEAD: https://marketplace.hw293.omantel.biz/redeem/?code=... served only 9 of 20 FRESH-TCP connections; the other 11 returned envoy 'no healthy upstream' (HTTP/1.1 503, server: envoy, body literally 'no healthy upstream'). Sampled with curl --no-keepalive so every request opens a NEW TCP connection — a keep-alive or HTTP/2 loop pins one backend and would have reported a clean 20/20, hiding this entirely. Both names resolve to the SINGLE VIP 212.72.24.49, so this is not DNS round-robin between regions but per-connection load-balancing across an upstream set in which about half the endpoints are unhealthy. The same ratio reproduces on every surface of this Sovereign: console apex 10/20 served, per-Org console 11/20 (its failure mode is a TLS reset at connect, curl 35, not a 503 — the per-Org listener/cert is absent from one backend rather than merely unhealthy), openova-mcp 10/20. A customer following a voucher link therefore fails roughly every other attempt. WHAT PASSES on the connections that are served: the redeem page returns 200, title 'Redeem voucher — OpenOva', ships the 6-step funnel nav with correct hrefs (1 Plan /plans · 2 Stack /apps · 3 Add-ons /addons · 4 Topology /bcp · 5 Review /review · 6 Checkout /checkout), and carries every outcome panel server-side — redeem-missing with a manual #redeem-manual-code input + Validate, redeem-not-valid, redeem-ended, redeem-valid, plus the #5634 redeem-throttled panel that keeps a 429 rate-limit from being mis-shown as a bad code. ONE GUARD DELIBERATELY NOT WRITTEN, recorded because the gap is intentional: the effect's `!org &&` half (a refetch must not clobber an operator's choice) is UNOBSERVABLE through this component - two candidates means no pre-select fires at all, one candidate means the select has a single option, and collapsing the list removes the operator's pick so the DOM resets it either way. A first cut slept 30ms and PASSED against both the guarded and unguarded code (the orgs query is staleTime 60s with no interval, so no refetch ever happened) - a check that cannot go red. Deleted rather than left as false coverage; the guard itself stays. THE OTHER OBSERVATION IN THIS ROW IS NOT A DEFECT: bp-agenity showing no '+ New instance' on the per-Org console is CORRECT - it is singleton-per-Org and already has an instance there, so the existing multiInstance!==false \|\| items.length===0 gate suppresses it by design (the walk said as much). REVISED 2026-08-03T21:53Z: the #5617 mechanism cited here is REFUTED on this env, so this row's remaining failure is NOT a Cilium egress denial. hubble observe --namespace uatco --verdict DROPPED --last 200 on cilium-cfspn (the agent on the node the gate pod actually runs on) returns ZERO dropped flows, with a CONTROL on the same agent and namespace showing live FORWARDED traffic — so the empty result is a real absence of drops, not a blind probe. The namespace also carries allow-gateway-and-apiserver with toEndpoints:[{}], an empty selector matching every endpoint incl. kube-dns. PR #5624 is still worth having (a workload should declare its own egress rather than lean on a broad namespace-wide allow), but it must not be described as fixing a DNS timeout this env does not exhibit. What remains unproven is the OAuth callback itself: only kube-probe health checks traversed the gate in the observed window. ROOT CAUSE NAMED 2026-08-04T05:2xZ (structural proof, deployed binary — verdict unchanged at ❌): the failure is #5617 and it is measurable without a browser. The constraining object in ns `uatco` is CiliumNetworkPolicy `allow-gateway-and-apiserver` with `endpointSelector: {}` — it selects EVERY pod in the Org namespace and declares an egress section, which under Cilium makes egress deny-by-default for all of them. It carries exactly 2 egress rules: `toEntities:[kube-apiserver]` on 443/6443, and `toEndpoints:[{}]` (same namespace). **Port-53 rules present: 0.** So cluster DNS is denied namespace-wide, the oidc-gate cannot resolve `keycloak.keycloak.svc.cluster.local`, and the OAuth callback 500s after a successful login. VACUITY CONTROL for the probe: the identical jq over all CiliumNetworkPolicies finds **5 policies that DO carry port-53 egress** (e.g. `catalyst-system/baseline-default-deny` dns_rules=2, `catalyst-system/openova-mcp-bp-openova-mcp-egress` dns_rules=2, `cnpg/cnpg-pair-bp-cnpg-pair-dr-failback-egress` dns_rules=2) — so a 0 for uatco is a real absence, not a probe that returns 0 for everything. Fix merged today as PR #5653 (per-Org baseline CNP gains 53/UDP+TCP to kube-system/kube-dns at L3/L4, plus bp-oidc-gate 0.1.9 shipping its own policy pair, plus `scripts/check-netpol-egress-completeness.py` as a fail-closed class gate over 36 charts / 109 policy documents). The Application-CR path resolves placement from platform/newapi/blueprint.yaml:259-261 (defaultPlacement.vcluster: mgmt, allowedPlacements host+mgmt) and therefore renders into a `mgmt` namespace no fresh prov has — that is the 201-then-Degraded experiment. MEASURED: `helm template` on platform/newapi/chart with exactly the values generateNewAPIHR stamps, with --api-versions external-secrets.io/v1beta1 so the chart's CRD gate is satisfied the way it is on a real host cluster, exits 1 with "catalystIntegration.externalSecret.remoteRef.key is empty". CONTROL: the same chart, same command, same api-versions, with ONLY the BSS door's catalystIntegration block added (organization_gitops.go stamps remoteRef.key sovereign/<fqdn>/newapi/<tenant>/admin-token) renders 1548 lines and exits 0 — so the failure is attributable to that one missing value and not to the invocation. CONSEQUENCE: the impliedHelmReleaseApps fix recorded above (openclaw implies newapi) now makes EVERY openclaw Org render a per-Org bp-newapi that will go InstallFailed on a template error. Stays ❌ — unchanged verdict, but the precondition story is replaced by a named source cause and fix site. |

Live condition text: Stalled:RetriesExceeded:Failed to install after 1 attempt(s) // Ready:InstallFailed:Helm install failed for release uatco/uatco-mail-rtz-a with chart bp-stalwart-tenant@0.1.13: 2 errors occurred: * admission webhook "validate.kyverno.svc-fail" denied the request: resource Service/uatco/uatco-mail-rtz-a-bp-stalwart-tenant-smtp was blocked due to the following policies forbid-nodeport-service: loadbalancer-must-disable . It IS deploy-gated, and the version arithmetic is exact. The Kyverno denial is real — forbid-nodeport-service / loadbalancer-must-disable-nodeport-allocation blocked Service uatco/uatco-mail-rtz-a-bp-stalwart-tenant-smtp for being type=LoadBalancer WITHOUT allocateLoadBalancerNodePorts=false, i.e. the §854 guard working exactly as designed and refusing a chart that would have silently allocated node ports. But the SOURCE chart already carries the fix: service-smtp.yaml:33 and service-imap.yaml:32 both set allocateLoadBalancerNodePorts: false under the §854/#5615 comment. Ancestry: commit 935d23842 (2026-08-04) 'fix(stalwart-tenant): §854 — disable nodePort allocation on SMTP/IMAP LB Services (0.1.14)' (#5625); source is now 0.1.15; hw292 runs 0.1.13 — ONE version short of the fix. So row 234 joins the deploy-gated set: the mail install fails because the env predates 0.1.14, not because the chart is broken. The §854 guard catching it before it could allocate a nodePort is the system behaving correctly. ‖ hw292-2026-08-09 BROWSER WALK — FUNNEL SURFACE ALIVE, CLAUSE NOT EXERCISED, STAYS ❌. MECHANISM, read in main rather than inferred: markPhase1Done in phase1_watch.go probes only under if outcome == helmwatch.OutcomeReady, so on this deployment (phase1Outcome=failed, 1 of 67 components) the console probe NEVER RAN and the honesty surface was never populated - the flag is absent because nothing looked, not because the door is well. What ships instead, re-read in main this session: products/catalyst/bootstrap/api/internal/handler/phase1_watch.go:1111-1112 runs the probe BEFORE the Phase-1 termination write; :1270-1275 records a probe failure as the NON-FATAL ConsoleDegraded + ConsoleDegradedDetail surface (the #3611 surface-not-gate idiom); :1277 then writes dep.Status = ready UNCONDITIONALLY, outside that if-block; and :1035-1068 re-probes in the background, clearing the flag once the door serves and warning terminally when attempts are exhausted, noting in the log line that the producer chain is NOT gated on it. So the mechanism this clause names no longer exists. The honesty requirement behind it does, and the replacement asserts it the way the platform can actually be held to: as an AGREEMENT between the record and the door, falsifiable in both directions on any converged prov, rather than as a latch that cost a topology. It was NOT deleted — deleting 13 rows would take the denominator to 273 and lift the score roughly three points on a day nothing passed, which is the floating-denominator behaviour the frozen 286 exists to prevent. The slot is reused for a clause that tests what the platform ACTUALLY does now, and the full swap (old clause, new clause, ground) is recorded in docs/ledger/uat-retirements.csv so it can be argued with.
